{\cal D}^{\prime}
on
w
, starting in
\iota^{\prime}
, and let
r=h(r^{\prime})
be the run of
{\cal D}
on
w
(that is defined by mapping each configuration of
r^{\prime}
to a state of
{\cal D}
via
h
).
Then,
r^{\prime}
should be accepting iff
r
is.

Recall that
r
is accepting iff
\it inf(r)=M
, for some
M=\{q_{1},\ldots q_{l}\}\in\alpha
. Hence,
r^{\prime}
should be accepting “according to
M
” iff
h(\it inf(r^{\prime}))=M
.
Thus,
r^{\prime}
should visit finitely often every configuration in
h^{-1}(Q\setminus M)
, and for
every

i\in[1..l]
, visit
some
configurations in
G_{i}=h^{-1}(q_{i})
infinitely often.

Since we should consider every choice of configurations in
G_{i}=h^{-1}(q_{i})
, where
|G_{i}|\leq m
, there are up to
2^{m}
choices for
G_{i}
, and therefore up to
(2^{m})^{n}
choices for
M
, each providing a Muller set
G
of configurations to be visited infinitely often.

As there are up to
2^{n}
sets in
\alpha
, we end up with up to
2^{n}(2^{m})^{n}\in 2^{O(mn)}
sets in
\alpha^{\prime}
.
∎

0.A.3
Proofs from
Section

4.2

See
4

Proof.

Observe first that there is no circularity in the definitions of the five reachability formulas, even though they are defined by each other: Formula 2 is defined on top of formula 1, which is defined on top of formulas 3 and 5, while formulas 3, 4, and 5 are defined with respect to reachability formulas over configurations of a lower level.

We prove the statement by induction on the level
m
of the configurations
S
,
B
,
T
in the reachability formulas. We split the proof to five cases corresponding to the five reachability formulas in
Table

1
. In order to clarify which equivalence of the induction hypothesis is used we denote by (I.H.1) the equivalence in
Table

1
for reachability formula 1, (I.H.2) the equivalence for reachability formula 2, and so on.

Reachability formula 1

(
m=0
): There is exactly one configuration of level
0
and it is the empty configuration. Thus
S=T=B=\langle\rangle
. We then derive:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}\langle\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&w\models(\neg\beta){\mathbf{U}}\tau\\[6.00006pt]
\iff&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ w_{[i..]}\models\tau\land\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta\\[6.00006pt]
\iff&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ \forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta\end{array}

(
m\rightarrow m+1
): Let
\langle S,s\rangle,\langle T,t\rangle,\langle B,b\rangle\in Q_{1}\times\cdots\times Q_{m+1}
be arbitrary configurations of level
m+1
. Thus we can use the equalities from
Table

1
for all configurations of
Q_{1}\times\cdots\times Q_{m}
as induction hypotheses. We need to show that:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\lor\langle S,s\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta)\end{array}

We split this into the (
\Rightarrow
)- and (
\Leftarrow
)-direction:

(
\Rightarrow
): We further refine this and first assume that the first disjunct is satisfied by
w
and defer the other case to a later point in the proof. We then apply (I.H.3) and derive:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta)\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \langle w[j],\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s))\\
\implies&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta)\end{array}

We now assume that the first disjunct is
not
satisfied by
w
and thus the second disjunct is satisfied by
w
. We then derive using (I.H.5):

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&\exists i_{1},i_{2}\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i_{1})})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i_{1}..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\exists j_{1}\in[0..i_{1}).\penalty\ \langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ \langle w[i_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..i_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..\max(i_{1}{-}1,i_{2})].\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta)\\
\implies&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta)\end{array}

(
\Leftarrow
): We assume that
w
satisfies the right-hand side of the equation and we instantiate
i
to be the smallest non-negative integer such that:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta)\end{array}

Assume that for all proper prefixes of
w_{[0..i)}
the combined letter stays in
s
, i.e.,
\langle w[j],\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
for all
j\in[0..i)
. We then apply (I.H.3) and obtain that
w
satisfies
\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
and so the left-hand side of the equation is satisfied by
w
. Thus let
k\in[0..i)
be the smallest non-negative integer such that:

\langle w[k],\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)

Since
k<i
, we immediately obtain one of the missing preconditions for applying (I.H.5):

\forall j_{2}\in[0..k].\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{2})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j_{2}..]}\not\models\beta

The second missing precondition is that we need to find a
j_{1}\in[0..i)
such that
\langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)
:
In the case
s\neq t
this is straightforward, since after reading
w_{[0..i)}
we reach
\langle T,t\rangle
, and thus there must be a
j_{1}<i
such that
\langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)
.
Thus let us assume
s=t
. In general there might be not such an index
j_{1}
. However, we have shown that
\langle w[k],\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
for some
k<i
and by the same reasoning as above there must be
j_{1}
between
k
and
i
such that
\langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)
.

We now can apply (I.H.5) and conclude this direction of the proof.

Reachability formula 2

We proceed by a straightforward derivation for which we use (I.H.1) in the second step:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{B(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{B(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{B(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{B(\beta)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}T\left(\tau\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&w\not\models S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{T(\tau)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{T(\tau)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{T(\tau)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{T(\tau)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}B\left(\beta\right)\\[8.99994pt]
\iff&\neg\big(\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(S,w_{[0..i)})=B\land w_{[i..]}\models\beta\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\neq T\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\tau)\big)\\
\iff&\forall i\geq 0.\penalty\ (\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})=B\land w_{[i..]}\models\beta)\\
&\qquad\qquad\rightarrow\penalty\ (\exists j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(S,w_{[0..j)})=T\land w_{[j..]}\models\tau)\end{array}

Reachability formula 3

We want to prove the following equivalence:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\
\iff&\exists i\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j_{1}\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{1})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j_{1}..]}\not\models\beta)\\
&\qquad\qquad\land\penalty\ (\forall j_{2}\in[0..i).\penalty\ \langle w[j_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s))\end{array}

(
\Rightarrow
): Assume that
w
satisfies the left-hand side of the equivalence. Notice that if
w
does not satisfy
\varphi\coloneqq\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{>0}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
, then necessarily
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..0)})=\langle T,t\rangle
and
w_{[0..]}\models\tau
and thus the right-hand side trivially holds for
i=0
.

Thus we can assume that
w
satisfies
\varphi
and using the same reasoning we know that either
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..0)})=\langle B,b\rangle
or
w_{[0..]}\models\beta
does not hold, which takes care of the case
j_{1}=0
in the second line of the right-hand side, assuming
i>0
.

Since we have
w\models\varphi
, there must be a
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
with
\delta(\langle T^{\prime},s\rangle,\sigma)=\langle T,t\rangle
such that the matching disjunct
\psi
of
\varphi
is satisfied by
w
. Note that this immediately implies that
s=t
. Observe that
\psi
is a conjunction of formulas with the shape

S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}T^{\prime}\left(\sigma\land{\mathbf{X}}\tau\right)

and we now apply (I.H.1) to all reachability formulas. Since they all share the same target, we can instantiate them to the same
i^{\prime}
(where
i^{\prime}
is the smallest non-negative integer satisfying the conditions) such that:

(a)

\delta(S,w_{[0..i^{\prime})})=T^{\prime}

(b)

w[i^{\prime}]=\sigma

(c)

w_{[i^{\prime}+1..]}\models\tau

(d)

For every
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
and every
j\in[0..i^{\prime})
at least one of the following statements holds:

(i)

\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\neq L

(ii)

w[j]\neq\eta

(e)

For every
\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
such that
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
and every
j\in[0..i^{\prime})
at least one of the following statements holds:

(i)

\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\neq B^{\prime}

(ii)

w[j]\neq\rho

(iii)

w_{[j+1..]}\not\models\beta

We now instantiate
i
of the right-hand side with
i^{\prime}+1
. Remember that we have
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
and together with (a-c) we obtain the first conjunct of the right hand side.

We now establish the third conjunct. Note that for every
j_{2}\in[0..i^{\prime})
we have
\langle w[j_{2}],\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
, since
\mathsf{Stay}(s)=(\Sigma\times Q_{1}\times\cdots\times Q_{m})\setminus\mathsf{Leave}(s)
and by (d) we do not encounter an element of
\mathsf{Leave}(s)
for any
j_{2}
. Further, for
j_{2}=i^{\prime}
we obtain
\langle w[j_{2}],\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
from (a,b) and the choice of
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
.

For the second conjunct it remains to show that for every
j_{1}\in[0..i)
:

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{1})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j_{1}..]}\not\models\beta

Assume that
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{1})})=\langle B,b\rangle
(if this is not the case we are immediately done). Due to the already established third conjunct, we have
\langle w[j_{1}-1],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1}-1)})\rangle=\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
and
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
. Thus (e,i) and (e,ii) cannot hold and (e,iii) must hold for
j_{1}(=j+1)
.

(
\Leftarrow
): We assume that
w
satisfies the right-hand side and instantiate
i
as the smallest
i\geq 0
such that:

(a)

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle

(b)

w_{[i..]}\models\tau

(c)

\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta

(d)

\forall j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \langle w[j],\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)

If
i=0
, then due to (a-b) we have immediately
w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
. Thus without loss of generality we can assume
i>0
from now on. Further, due to (c) we have that either
\langle S,s\rangle\neq\langle B,b\rangle
or
w\not\models\beta
. Thus it remains to show that
w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{>0}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
.
Define
\sigma
as
w[i-1]
and
T^{\prime}
as
\delta(S,w_{[0..i-1)})
. From (a,d) we then follow:

(e)

\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)

(f)

\delta(S,w_{[0..i-1)})=T^{\prime}
and
\delta(\langle T^{\prime},s\rangle,\sigma)=\langle T,t\rangle

(g)

w_{[i-1..]}\models\sigma\land{\mathbf{X}}\tau

We are going to use the induction hypothesis to show that
w
satisfies the disjunct corresponding to
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle
. The first conjunct is satisfied from (f,g) and (I.H.1). From (d) it follows that for every
j\in[0..i)
the tuple
\langle w[j],\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\rangle
is not in
\mathsf{Leave}(s)
. Thus for every
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
, we either have
w_{[j..]}\not\models\eta
or
\delta(S,w_{[0..j)})\neq L
. Thus by (I.H.1) and (f,g) we obtain that
w
satisfies the second conjunct. Analogously, from (c) it follows that for every
j\in[1..i)
and for every
\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
such that
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
, either
\delta(S,w_{[0..j-1)})\neq B^{\prime}
or
w_{[j-1..]}\not\models\rho\land{\mathbf{X}}\beta
. Thus by (I.H.1) and (f,g) we obtain that
w
satisfies the third and final conjunct.

Reachability formula 4

We want to prove the following equivalence:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[18.00005pt]
\iff&\forall i\geq 0.\penalty\ \big((\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle B,b\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\beta)\\
&\phantom{\forall i\geq 0.\penalty\ }\qquad\lor(i>0\wedge\langle w[i{-}1],\delta(S,w_{[0..i{-}1)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s))\big)\\
&\phantom{\forall i\geq 0.\penalty\ }\rightarrow\penalty\ (\exists j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[j..]}\models\tau)\end{array}

(
\Rightarrow
): Assume that
w
satisfies the left-hand side of the equivalence. Then we immediately obtain from the definition that either
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..0)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle
or
w_{[0..]}\not\models\beta
. Further, notice that if
w
does not satisfy
\varphi\coloneqq\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak},>0}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
, then necessarily
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..0)})=\langle T,t\rangle
and
w_{[0..]}\models\tau
, and thus the right-hand side trivially holds for all
i
by instantiating
j
with
0
.

Thus we can assume that
w
satisfies
\varphi
and using the fact that at least one of
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..0)})=\langle B,b\rangle
and
w_{[0..]}\models\beta
does not hold, we only need to prove the right-hand side for
i>0
.

Since we have
w\models\varphi
, then either
w
satisfies the disjunct
\psi
in line (2) or there exists a
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
with
\delta(\langle T^{\prime},s\rangle,\sigma)=\langle T,t\rangle
such that the matching disjunct
\chi
in (1) is satisfied by
w
. We assume that the first case holds, and defer the second case to later.

Note, that when applying the induction hypothesis (I.H.2) to each conjunct of
\psi
, we can simplify the nested existential quantification to
{\mathbf{false}}
, since
w\not\models{\mathbf{false}}
. Thus we obtain for all
i\geq 0
:

(a)

For every
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
at least one of the following statements holds:

(i)

\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\neq L

(ii)

w[i]\neq\eta

(b)

For every
\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
such that
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
at least one of the following statements holds:

(i)

\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\neq B^{\prime}

(ii)

w[i]\neq\rho

(iii)

w_{[i+1..]}\not\models\beta

Because
w
and the sequence of generated configurations does not contain a combined letter that leaves
s
due to (a), we do have
\langle w[i],\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\rangle\notin\mathsf{Leave}(s)
for all
i\geq 0
. Thus
\langle w[i],\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
for all
i\geq 0
. Further, due to (b) we either do not see a combined letter at position
i
that enters the configuration
\langle B,b\rangle
(and thus
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[i+1..]})=\langle B,b\rangle
) or the infinite suffix of
w_{[i+1..]}
does not satisfy
\beta
. From this, we can conclude that the right-hand side holds. (The case for
i=0
was already established in the second paragraph.)

We now assume that there exists a tuple
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle
such that
w
satisfies the matching disjunct
\chi
of line (1). Note that the existence of
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle
immediately implies
s=t
. Moreover, we can assume that
w
does not satisfy the disjunct
\psi
from line (2). Thus by applying (I.H.2), we know that there exists an
k\geq 0
such that either
\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})=L
and
w[k]=\eta
from
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
or
\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})=B
,
w[k]=\rho
, and
w_{[k+1..]}\models\beta
for some
\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
and
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
. Thus when we apply the (I.H.2) to the corresponding reachability formula of
\chi
we obtain a
k^{\prime}\in[0..k)
such that
\delta(S,w_{[0..k^{\prime})})=T^{\prime}
and
w_{[k^{\prime}..]}\models\sigma\land{\mathbf{X}}\tau
. Observe that
\chi
is a conjunction of formulas with the shape

S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\cdot(\cdot)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\text{\tiny weak}$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}T^{\prime}\left(\sigma\land{\mathbf{X}}\tau\right)

and we now apply the induction hypothesis to all reachability formulas. Since they all share the same “target”, we can instantiate the nested existential quantification to the same
j^{\prime}
(where
j^{\prime}
is the smallest non-negative integer satisfying the condition) such that:

(a)

\delta(S,w_{[0..j^{\prime})})=T^{\prime}

(b)

w[j^{\prime}]=\sigma

(c)

w_{[j^{\prime}+1..]}\models\tau

(d)

For every
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
and every
i^{\prime}\in[0..j^{\prime}]
at least one of the following statements holds:

(i)

\delta(S,w_{[0..i^{\prime})})\neq L

(ii)

w[i^{\prime}]\neq\eta

(e)

For every
\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
such that
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
and every
i^{\prime}\in[0..j^{\prime}]
at least one of the following statements holds:

(i)

\delta(S,w_{[0..i^{\prime})})\neq B^{\prime}

(ii)

w[i^{\prime}]\neq\rho

(iii)

w_{[i^{\prime}+1..]}\not\models\beta

In order to show that
w
satisfy the right-hand side of the equation, let now
i\geq 0
be an arbitrary integer and we need to prove:

\begin{array}[]{c}\big((\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle B,b\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\beta)\lor(i>0\land\langle w[i{-}1],\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s))\big)\\
\rightarrow(\exists j\in[0..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[j..]}\models\tau)\end{array}

The case
i=0
was already discussed in the second paragraph. Further, if
i>j^{\prime}+1
, then (a-c) show that
j
can be instantiated with
j^{\prime}+1
. Thus assume that
i\in[1..(j^{\prime}+1)]
. Further, since
j^{\prime}
was chosen to be the smallest integer such that (a-c) holds, we can simplify the expression we need to prove to:

(\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[i..]}\not\models\beta)\land\langle w[i{-}1],\delta(S,w_{[0..i{-}1)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s))

Note that second conjunct is direct consequence of (d). For the first conjunct, we proceed by contradiction and assume that
i
is the smallest integer such that:

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle B,b\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\beta

Due to the already established second conjunct, we have
\langle w[i-1],\delta(S,w_{[0..i-1)}\rangle)=\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
and
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
. But since
i-1\in[0..j^{\prime}]
, we have a contradiction to (e).

(
\Leftarrow
): We assume that
w
satisfies the right-hand side.
We first consider the case that the nested existential quantification is not true for any
i\geq 0
. Thus for all
i\geq 0
it holds that:

(a)

\langle w[i],\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)

(b)

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle
or
w_{[i..]}\not\models\beta

Since
w
satisfies the right-hand side, it is easy to see that we have either
\langle S,s\rangle\neq\langle B,b\rangle
or
w\not\models\beta
. Hence it remains to show that
w\models\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak},>0}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
, which we do by showing that
w
satisfies (2). From (a,b) we obtain:

(a’)

For every
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
we have either
\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\neq L
or
w[i]\neq\eta
.

(b’)

Either
w_{[i+1..]}\not\models\beta
or for every
\langle\rho,B^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
such that
\delta(\langle B^{\prime},s\rangle,\rho)=\langle B,b\rangle
we have either
\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\neq B^{\prime}
or
w[i]\neq\rho
.

We then apply the induction hypothesis (I.H.2) to (a’,b’) and obtain that
w
satisfies the disjunct of line (2).

We now consider the second case and assume that there is a
j
such that:

(a)

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle T,t\rangle

(b)

w_{[j..]}\models\tau

Without loss of generality, we can assume
j
to be the smallest integer with such a property. Further, since
w
satisfies the right-hand side, we have:

(c)

\forall i\in[0..j).\penalty\ \langle w[i],\delta(S,w_{[0..i)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)

(d)

\forall i\in[0..j].\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[i..]}\not\models\beta

If
j=0
, then
w
immediately satisfies the left-hand side. Thus assume
j>0
. Due to (c), we have that
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle=\langle w_{[j-1]},\delta(S,w_{[0..j-1)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
and
\delta(\langle\delta(S,w_{[0..j-1)}),s\rangle,w_{[j-1]})=\langle T,t\rangle
. Thus there exists a matching disjunct for
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle
in line (1) and we know it is satisfied by
w
due the
(a,b,d)
and the induction hypothesis (I.H.2).

Reachability formula 5

We want to prove the following equivalence:

\begin{array}[]{cl}&w\models\langle S,s\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)\\[18.00005pt]
\iff&\exists i_{1},i_{2}\geq 0.\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i_{1})})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i_{1}..]}\models\tau\\
&\phantom{\exists i_{1},i_{2}\geq 0.\penalty\ }\land\penalty\ (\exists j_{1}\in[0..i_{1}).\penalty\ \langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1}))}\in\mathsf{Enter}(s)\rangle)\\
&\phantom{\exists i_{1},i_{2}\geq 0.\penalty\ }\land\penalty\ \langle w[i_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..i_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)\\
&\phantom{\exists i_{1},i_{2}\geq 0.\penalty\ }\land\penalty\ (\forall j_{2}\in[0..\max(i_{1}{-}1,i_{2})].\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{2})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j_{2}..]}\not\models\beta)\end{array}

(
\Rightarrow
): We assume that

w\models\langle S,s\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}$}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)

holds and proceed by first constructing a witness for
i_{2}
and then one for
i_{1}
.

(
\exists i_{2}
):
Since
w
satisfies line (3), there must be a combined letter
\langle\sigma,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
such that
w
satisfies the corresponding disjunct. We apply to this disjunct the induction hypothesis (I.H.3) and instantiate the existential quantifier to
i_{2}
(which we intend to be the witness for
\exists i_{2}
) with the following properties:

1.

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i_{2})})=\langle L,s\rangle

2.

w[i_{2}]=\sigma
and
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i_{2})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[i_{2}..]}\not\models\beta

3.

\forall j_{2}\in[0..i_{2}).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{2})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j_{2}..]}\not\models\beta

4.

\forall j_{2}\in[0..i_{2}).\penalty\ \langle w[j_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
.

Observe that due to (1,2) we have
\langle w[i_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..i_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
and together with (2,3) one can see that
i_{2}
is a sufficient witness for the
\exists i_{2}
in the right-hand side of the equation we want to prove. Note that property (4) is not useless and will be of importance later.

(
\exists i_{1}
):
Since
w
also satisfies lines (1,2), there must be a combined letter
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)
such that
w
satisfies the corresponding disjunct. We then apply the induction hypothesis (I.H.1) to all terms of the matching conjunction in lines (1,2). Since all reachability formulas share the same target configuration and formula, we can instantiate all existential quantifiers to the same integer
k
by picking the smallest instance for each existential quantifier. Let now
k
be this smallest non-negative integer. We introduce the following two abbreviations
T^{\prime\prime}=\delta(\langle T^{\prime},\cdot\rangle,\sigma)
and
R^{\prime}_{\eta}=\delta(\langle R,\cdot\rangle,\eta)
for some
\langle\eta,R\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(b)
. Note that we can leave out the last state in the definition of
T^{\prime\prime}
, since for all states
q,p
the transition relation maps the the same successor configuration, i.e.,
\delta(\langle T^{\prime},q\rangle,\sigma)=\delta(\langle T^{\prime},p\rangle,\sigma)
. Analogously, for we omit it from
R^{\prime}_{\eta}
. We now list all relevant properties of
k
derived from applying the induction hypothesis:

5.

\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})=T^{\prime}

6.

w[k]=\sigma

7.

w_{[k+1..]}\models T^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)

8.

For all
\langle\eta,R\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(b)
and all
\ell\in[0..k)
at least one of the following statements is true:

(a)

\delta(S,w_{[0..\ell)})\neq R

(b)

w[\ell]\neq\eta

(c)

w_{[\ell+1..]}\not\models R^{\prime}_{\eta}\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)

We continue and apply the induction hypothesis to (7) and then obtain
i_{1}
(which we intend to be the witness for
\exists i_{1}
) with the following properties (already shifted to indices relative to
w
). Further, we can assume
i_{1}
to be the smallest integer with these properties.

9.

\delta(T^{\prime\prime},w_{[k+1..i_{1})})=\langle T,t\rangle
and
w_{[i_{1}..]}\models\tau

10.

\forall j\in[(k+1)..i_{1}).\penalty\ \delta(T^{\prime\prime},w_{[k+1..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta

11.

\forall j\in[(k+1)..i_{1}).\penalty\ \langle w[j],\delta(\delta(T^{\prime},\sigma),w_{[k+1..j)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(t)

From (5,6,9) we obtain that
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i_{1})})=\langle T,t\rangle
and that
w_{[i_{1}..]}\models\tau
taking care of the first line of the right-hand side of the equation. Further, by construction
\langle w[k],\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})\rangle=\langle\sigma,T\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)
and since
k<i_{1}
, we now know that the second line is satisfied by our witness for
\exists i_{1}
. Next, we inline the definition of
T^{\prime\prime}
in (10) and obtain:
\forall j\in[(k+1)..i_{1}).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta
. Thus in order to complete this direction of the proof it remains to show that:

\forall j\in[(i_{2}+1)..k].\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j..]}\not\models\beta

If
[(i_{2}+1)..k]
is the empty set, we are done. Thus assume that
i_{2}<k
. We proceed with a proof by contradiction and assume that there exists an index
j\in[i_{2}+1..k]
such that
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle B,b\rangle
and
w_{[j..]}\models\beta
. Due to (1) and (4), there must be some
j^{\prime}\in[i_{2}..j{-}1]
such that the combined letter
\langle\eta,R\rangle=\langle w[j^{\prime}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j^{\prime})})\rangle
is in
\mathsf{Enter}(b)
. Without loss of generality we can further assume that:

12.

\forall j^{\prime\prime}\in[j^{\prime}..j).\penalty\ \langle w[j^{\prime\prime}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j^{\prime\prime})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(b)

Note that
j^{\prime}<k
and since (8a) and (8b) cannot be true we can instantiate (8c) to:

w_{[j^{\prime}+1..]}\not\models R^{\prime}_{\eta}\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)

We now apply the induction hypothesis to this and obtain an index
\ell\geq j^{\prime}+1
such that the following two statements hold (already with adjusted indices):

13.

At least one of the following statements hold:

(a)

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..\ell)})=\langle T,t\rangle\wedge w_{[\ell..]}\models\tau

(b)

\ell>j^{\prime}+1\wedge\langle w[\ell-1],\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..\ell-1)}\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(b)

14.

\forall j^{\prime\prime}\in[(j^{\prime}{+}1)..\ell).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j^{\prime\prime})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j^{\prime\prime}..]}\not\models\beta

Since
j^{\prime}<j
and since we assumed that
j
is the smallest integer satisfying
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle B,b\rangle
and
w_{[j^{\prime\prime}..]}\models\beta
, we can conclude with (14) that
\ell\leq j
. However, we have a contradiction between (13a),
\ell\leq j\leq k<i_{1}
, and
i_{1}
being the smallest index reaching
\langle T,t\rangle
and satisfying
\tau
. Thus (13b) must hold. However, since
\ell\leq j
this contradicts (12). Thus there cannot be such a
j
and we can conclude this direction.

(
\Leftarrow
): Assume that
w
satisfies the left-hand side. Then there exists
i_{1},i_{2},j_{1}\geq 0
, further let
i_{1},i_{2}
be the smallest integers and let
j_{1}
be the largest integer such that:

(a)

\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i_{1})})=\langle T,t\rangle

(b)

w_{[i_{1}..]}\models\tau

(c)

j_{1}<i_{1}

(d)

\langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)

(e)

\langle w[i_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..i_{2})})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)

(f)

\forall j_{2}\in[0..\max(i_{1}-1,i_{2})].\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j_{2})})\neq\langle B,b\rangle\lor w_{[j_{2}..]}\not\models\beta

Since
i_{2}
is the smallest integer with this property, we have
\langle w[k],\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
for all
k\in[0..i_{2}{-}1]
. Thus we can apply (I.H.3) to this and (e,f) to show that
w
satisfies line (3) for
\langle w[i_{2}],\delta(S,w_{[0..i_{2})})\rangle=\langle\sigma,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(s)
. In order to show the disjunct of (1) and (2), we need to identify a suitable
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(t)
. We argue that
\langle\sigma,T^{\prime}\rangle=\langle w[j_{1}],\delta(S,w_{[0..j_{1})})\rangle
is a suitable choice.

We proceed by first showing that
w
satisfies line (1). Since
j_{1}
is the largest integer with such a property, we immediately get
\langle w[k],\delta(S,w_{[0..k)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Stay}(s)
for all
k\in[j_{1}{+}1,i_{1}{-}1]
. By applying (I.H.3) we get:

w_{[j_{1}+1..]}\models\delta(\langle T^{\prime},\cdot\rangle,\sigma)\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)

We now apply (I.H.1) and use (d) to establish that the first line is satisfied by
w
. In order show that line (2) is satisfied it remains to show that for all
k\in[0..j_{1}]
and all
\langle\eta,L\rangle\in\mathsf{Enter}(b)
either
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..k)})\neq\langle\eta,L\rangle
or
w_{[k..]}\not\models\eta\land{\mathbf{X}}\Big(\delta(\langle R,\cdot\rangle,\eta)\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)\Big)
.

We proceed by contradiction. Assume that there is
k\leq j_{1}
such that
\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,

w_{[0..k)})=\langle\eta,L\rangle
,
w[k]=\eta
, and
w_{[k+1..]}\models\delta(\langle R,\cdot\rangle,\eta)\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right))
. By applying (I.H.4) and adjusting the indices we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\forall i\geq k+1.&\big((\delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..i)})=\langle T,t\rangle\land w_{[i..]}\models\tau)\\
&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \lor\penalty\ (i>k+1\land\langle w[i{-}1],\delta(S,w_{[0..i{-}1)})\rangle\in\mathsf{Leave}(b))\big)\\
&\rightarrow\penalty\ (\exists j\in[k+1..i).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle B,b\rangle\land w_{[j..]}\models\beta)\end{array}

By instantiating this with (a,b) we get:

\exists j\in[k+1..i_{1}).\penalty\ \delta(\langle S,s\rangle,w_{[0..j)})=\langle B,b\rangle\land w_{[j..]}\models\beta

However this contradicts (f).

Thus we can apply (I.H.1) to establish that
w
satisfies (2).
∎

See
5

Proof.

Observe first that there is no circularity in the definitions of the five reachability formulas, even though they are defined by each other: Formula 2 is defined on top of formula 1, which is defined on top of formulas 3 and 5, while formulas 3, 4, and 5 are defined with respect to reachability formulas over configurations of a lower level.

Let
i\geq 1
be an arbitrary index. We prove the statement by induction on the level
m
of the configurations
S
,
B
,
T
in the reachability formulas. We split the proof to five cases corresponding to the five reachability formulas.

Reachability formula 1.

Let
\beta\in\Pi_{i}
and let
\tau\in\Sigma_{i}
. We proceed by an induction on the the configuration level
m
.

(
m=0
): There is only one configuration of level
0
and it is the empty configuration. Thus
S=T=B=\langle\rangle
. Applying the definition we obtain:

.

We now can use apply the induction hypothesis and obtain that the formula is in

.

and let

. Then by induction hypothesis

Reachability formula 3.

Let
\beta\in\Pi_{i}
and let
\tau\in\Sigma_{i}
. Note that under this assumption
{\mathbf{false}}
,
\eta
, and
\rho\land{\mathbf{X}}\beta
belong to
\Pi_{i}
and that
\sigma\land{\mathbf{X}}\tau
belongs to
\Sigma_{i}
. Thus by applying the induction hypothesis we obtain that
\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{>0}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
is in
\Sigma_{i}
. Since
\neg\beta
also belongs to
\Sigma_{i}
, we obtain that
\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
is from
\Sigma_{i}
.

Reachability formula 4.

Let
\beta\in\Sigma_{i}
and let
\tau\in\Pi_{i}
. Note that under this assumption
\eta
and
\rho\land{\mathbf{X}}\beta
belong to
\Sigma_{i}
and that
{\mathbf{false}}
,
\neg\beta
, and
\sigma\land{\mathbf{X}}\tau
belong to
\Pi_{i}
. We then apply again the induction hypothesis and obtain that
\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak},>0}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
and thus also
\langle S,s\rangle\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)
belongs to
\Pi_{i}
.

Reachability formula 5.

Let
\beta\in\Pi_{i}
and let
\tau\in\Sigma_{i}
. By an analogous argument to the two preceding cases we obtain that the formulas in line (1) and (3) belong to
\Sigma_{i}
. Further, note that by the induction hypothesis
\eta\land{\mathbf{X}}\Big(\delta(\langle R,\cdot\rangle,\eta)\xrightarrow[\bcancel{\langle T,t\rangle\left(\tau\right)}]{\text{\tiny weak}}\langle B,b\rangle\left(\beta\right)\Big)
belongs to
\Pi_{i}
and thus the overall reachability formula 5 belongs to
\Sigma_{i}
.
∎

0.A.4
Proofs from
Section

4.3

See
6

Proof.

We first prove the upper bound on the depth of the formula and then move on to the claim about the length of the formula.

Depth Analysis.

We prove the claim by induction on the level
i
.
For the base case of
i=0
, the main reachability formula is just
(\neg\beta){\mathbf{U}}\tau
, having
\mathsf{D}(0,d)=d+1
, which is equal to
d+3^{0}
, as required.
For the induction step of a general level
i>0
, we will first establish a bound on
\mathsf{D}(i,d)
that is relative to
\mathsf{D}(i{-}1,d)
, and then get from it an absolute bound, using the induction hypothesis.

Observe that
\mathsf{D}(i,d)
is bounded in
Table

2
to the maximum between
\mathsf{D}_{3}(i,d)
and
\mathsf{D}_{5}(i,d)
, while the bound on
\mathsf{D}_{5}(i,d)
is at least as on
\mathsf{D}_{3}(i,d)
. Hence:

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(i,d)\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \mathsf{D}_{5}(i,d)\penalty\

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(i{-}1,\max(1+\mathsf{D}_{3}(i,d),1+\mathsf{D}_{4}(i,d)))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(i{-}1,1+\mathsf{D}(i{-}1,d+1))

Applying the induction hypothesis on
\mathsf{D}(i{-}1,d)
, we get an absolute bound:

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(i,d)\penalty\

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}(i{-}1,1+(d+1+3^{i{-}1}))\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+d+3^{i{-}1}+3^{i{-}1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ d+3^{i}

Length Analysis.

We prove the claim by induction on the level
i
.
For the base case of
i=0
, the main reachability formula is just
(\neg\beta){\mathbf{U}}\tau
, having
\mathsf{L}(0,l)=2+2l
, which is indeed not larger than
l\cdot(10|\Sigma|^{2}n)^{4^{i}}
.
For the induction step of a general level
i>0
, we will first establish a bound on
\mathsf{L}(i,l)
relative to
\mathsf{L}(i{-}1,\cdot)
, and then apply the induction hypothesis to get an absolute bound.

Observe that
\mathsf{L}(i,l)
is bounded in
Table

2
with respect to
\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,\cdot)
and
\mathsf{L}_{5}(i,\cdot)
, and

\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l)
is already bounded in
Table

2
with respect to
\mathsf{L}(i{-}1,\cdot)
.
We simplify the
\mathsf{L}_{5}
bound, and substitute the bound on
\mathsf{L}_{3}
, getting:

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{5}(i,l)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Sigma|n^{i-1}\cdot\big(\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l))+2+

\displaystyle|\Sigma|n^{i-1}\cdot\big(\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,\max(3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l),3+\mathsf{L}_{4}(i,l)))+1\big)\big)+

\displaystyle 1+|\Sigma|n^{i-1}\cdot(1+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,3+l))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Sigma|n^{i-1}\cdot\big(\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l))+2+

\displaystyle|\Sigma|n^{i-1}\cdot\big(\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l))+1\big)\big)+

\displaystyle 1+|\Sigma|n^{i-1}\cdot(1+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,3+l))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l))+

\displaystyle|\Sigma|n^{(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l))+

\displaystyle|\Sigma|n^{(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,3+l))+

\displaystyle|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}+

\displaystyle 3|\Sigma|n^{(i-1)}+1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,3+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l+3))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,12+2l+4|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i{-}1,l{+}3))

We can now bound
\mathsf{L}(i,l)
relative to
\mathsf{L}(i{-}1,\cdot)
, getting:

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(i,l)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1+\mathsf{L}_{3}(i,l)+\mathsf{L}_{5}(i,l)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4+2l+4|\Sigma|n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i{-}1,l{+}3)+

\displaystyle 4|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,12+2l+4|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i{-}1,l{+}3))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,12+2l+4|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i{-}1,l{+}3))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i{-}1,l{+}3))

Applying the induction hypothesis on
\mathsf{L}(i{-}1,\cdot)
, we get an absolute bound:

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(i,l)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i-1,5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}\mathsf{L}_{1}(i{-}1,l{+}3))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}(5|\Sigma|^{2}n^{2(i-1)}(l+3)(2|\Sigma|n)^{4^{i-1}})(10|\Sigma|^{2}n)^{4^{i-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 25|\Sigma|^{4}n^{4(i-1)}(l+3)(10|\Sigma|^{2}n)^{2\cdot 4^{i-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle l(10|\Sigma|^{2}n)^{4^{i}}

The last inequality clearly holds for
i\geq 2
as the
(10|\Sigma|^{2}n)^{4^{i}}
term becomes very large.
For
i=1
, it also holds as the
n^{4(i-1)}
term disappears and we get
25\cdot 10^{2}(l+3)\leq 10^{4}ln^{2}
, which holds even for
l=n=1
.
∎

04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}C(\neg(C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,>0$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,>0$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,>0$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,>0$}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}C))\in\Sigma_{2}
. Thus
{\mathit{Fin}}(C)\in\Sigma_{2}
.
∎

See
4.1

Proof.

We complete the proof of the Theorem and give a complete analysis of all six acceptance conditions:

•

{\cal D}
is a Muller automaton: the overall formula
\varphi
is in
\Delta_{2}
, since it is a Boolean combination of
{\mathit{Fin}}(C)
formulas, which by
Lemma

7
belong to
\Sigma_{2}
.

•

{\cal D}
is a coBüchi automaton: we construct the formula
\varphi
directly from the coBüchi condition
\alpha
, having a conjunction of
{\mathit{Fin}}(C)
formulas, over all configurations
C
that are mapped to states in
\alpha
. As
{\mathit{Fin}}(C)
belongs to
\Sigma_{2}
, so does
\varphi
.

•

{\cal D}
is a Büchi automaton: we can complement it, apply the above argument over the resulting coBüchi automaton, and negate the resulting formula to obtain a formula from
\Pi_{2}
.

•

{\cal D}
is a looping-coBüchi automaton: Let
s\in Q
be the unique sink state that all accepting runs end up in, and let
S
be the set of configurations mapped to
s
. We then define
\varphi
as
\bigvee_{C\in S}\text{\Large$\iota$}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.76031pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}C
. Note that
\varphi
belongs to
\Sigma_{1}
since every disjunct belongs to
\Sigma_{1}
due to
Lemma

5
.

•

{\cal D}
is a looping-Büchi automaton: the dual of the previous argument.

•

{\cal D}
is a weak automaton: Let
G\subseteq Q
be an accepting SCC of
{\cal D}
and
G^{\prime}\subseteq Q
be all states that are reachable from
G
, but are not in
G
. Let
H
and
H^{\prime}
be the set of configurations that are mapped to
G
and
G^{\prime}
, respectively. Then by
Lemma

4
, we have that
(\bigvee_{C\in H}\text{\Large$\iota$}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}C)\land(\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}}\neg\text{\Large$\iota$}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\displaystyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\textstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptstyle{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt\hskip-0.3pt\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{\hskip-0.89449pt\hbox{{$\thicksim$}}}}}\hskip 0.2pt}\limits_{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ $}}}\mathrel{\hskip-1.2pt\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}\!\!\scriptscriptstyle{}}}C^{\prime})
exactly captures all words that are accepted by eventually ending up in
G
. The overall formula
\varphi
is then the disjunction of these formulas constructed for each accepting SCC of
{\cal D}
. The membership in
\Delta_{1}
then follows immediately from
Lemma

5
. ∎
